Peanut Peptide Injections Promising for Allergy Immunotherapy

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

— A novel route to immunotherapy for peanut allergy passes phase II test

A novel peptide-based immunotherapy for peanut allergy appeared safe and modestly efficacious for reducing children's symptoms over the first 12 months, a phase II trial showed.

For the primary efficacy endpoint, the higher of two monthly PVX108 shot dosages significantly boosted the maximum tolerated dose of peanut from baseline to month 12 by 2.4-fold, compared with a 1.2-fold change in the placebo group, reported Brian P. Vickery, MD, of Emory University and chief of the Division of Allergy/Immunology at Children's Healthcare of Atlanta, at the American College of Allergy, Asthma & Immunology annual meeting.

In terms of safety, there were no cases of anaphylaxis or other treatment-related allergic or severe adverse events with the shot. Only six of the 71 trial participants reported any adverse event (13% with the higher dose tested), largely local administration site reactions.

At an oral food challenge at 1 year, 39% of patients on the higher dose tolerated at least 300 mg of peanut protein compared with 17% in the placebo group (the equivalent of one peanut). "This is more than double, on average, what causes accidental reactions in the field when people are exposed accidentally while they're trying to avoid peanut. And it's been shown in previous work that ... is probably clinically meaningful for most patients with peanut allergy," Vickery said. But he acknowledged it "is probably less than what you'd expect in a study of oral immunotherapy, where the participants are swallowing about 300 mg of protein daily as part of their treatment."

While efficacy needs to be confirmed in a larger trial, "there's also a big safety difference and a difference in burden in terms of daily versus monthly therapy," he added, pointing to adverse events in most immunotherapy patients with other routes of administration and anaphylaxis in 10-15% of trial participants in some studies.

Immunotherapy shots offer an advantage of fewer office visits than the typically once-every-2-week schedule for oral immunotherapy up-dosing and also not requiring kids to consume peanut products daily, commented Jay Lieberman, MD, of the University of Tennessee Health Science Center in Memphis. With peanut protein, though, "you can't do it the same way we do allergy shots to cat or oak tree pollen, because it is too dangerous. It caused too many reactions," he said.

PVX108 comprises seven short peptides representing immunodominant T-cell epitopes of major peanut allergens rather than intact peanut proteins that interact with the immune system and can lead to reactions. "This is designed to get around that problem by formulating fragments of peanut proteins that are too small to interact with IgE [immunoglobulin E] and the allergic components of the immune system and instead interact directly with T cells, which are thought to be kind of the driving force behind the allergy," Vickery said.

"We have made some progress in being able to treat peanut allergy with oral immunotherapy and now FDA-approved omalizumab [Xolair] as the two most commonly used options," Vickery said in an interview monitored by media relations for drug developer Aravax. "But there are millions of patients out there with peanut allergy, and most of them are not being treated due to limitations in care and also limitations in access to these treatments and because of limitations in the treatments themselves."

"The scientific community believes that there is plenty of room for new forms of immunotherapy to be developed," he added, "because we don't think of peanut allergy as a one-size-fits-all condition where one treatment will solve the problem."

While the only FDA-approved peanut oral immunotherapy product recently left the market as clinicians rely on off-the-shelf commercial products, Lieberman agreed that PVX108 could find a place: "Bringing an [oral immunotherapy] FDA-approved thing to the market when people are already just using PB2 powder [commercially available peanut flour], that was the problem."

The trial included 71 peanut allergy patients ages 4 to 11 years at 14 trial sites in the U.S. and Australia in the primary efficacy and safety cohort. They were randomized to double-blind treatment with 5-nmol or 50-nmol PVX108 or placebo given monthly by intradermal administration over 12 months. At the end of that period, a double-blind placebo-controlled food challenge was done following the PRACTALL guidelines.

After 6 months off treatment, a follow-up food challenge was conducted at month 18. It showed evidence of maintenance or improvement in clinical effects compared with month 12, indicating disease-modifying potential, Vickery said, although this needs further confirmation.

The optimal length of treatment isn't clear yet, but immunotherapy shots for other allergens typically take 3 to 5 years of treatment, Vickery noted. "So it's likely that longer-term treatment might be necessary or even might be more optimal."

How Does The REO Runabout Stack Up Against Slate?

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Browse this and many other websites without banner ads, personalized tracking and video ads.

Like the idea of a Slate but don't want an EV? Well, an upstart from Dallas, Texas has just the thing for you. That is, if it ever becomes a reality. Startup company REO recently unveiled renderings of its new truck, dubbed the Runabout. It's a similar idea to Slate, with just one basic frame, three different body variants to choose from, no frills, a bunch of customization, and a sub-$30,000 price tag. The main difference is that that Runabout uses a four-cylinder gas engine, rather than electric motors. So how does it compare to Slate?

First, let's look at price. The most basic Slate you can get—cheekily called "The Blank Slate"—costs $24,950, and it's a two-door pickup with nothing more than some seats, a steering wheel, a battery, and a single rear electric motor to get you around. There's no radio, power windows, or screens to speak of.

The cheapest REO you can get will be the Runabout T4X, which costs $21,500*. Note the asterisk, as REO says the current listed price is an estimate, and the company has no factory or clear plans for getting it to market. For that shockingly low price, it's estimated that you'll get a four-cylinder engine, a choice of either a six-speed manual or automatic transmission, and mechanical four-wheel drive. It's a two-door, two-seat pickup with drop-down sides for the bed, like a Kei truck.

What four-cylinder engine will the Runabout have and which manufacturer will supply it? REO isn't saying. There are far more questions than answers so far. REO does say that it has a "targeted powertrain life of 500,000 miles." To quote myself reading that in the press release: "lol."

While Slate uses a hybrid body-on-frame style chassis, the REO has an old-school ladder frame, like a classic pickup. It's similar to slate in that it offers modular body styles, but with key differences. There's a two-door pickup and an SUV version, like Slate, but there's also a four-door pickup, which Slate doesn't have. REO claims its truck has solid rear axles, too, to give it more off-road credibility.

The design isn't bad, even if it looks like a Grand Theft Auto version of an Australian-market Land Cruiser, but that's to be expected. REO hired longtime automotive designer Frank Stephenson, whose previous work includes the first BMW-owned Mini Cooper, the Maserati MC12, and the McLaren P1. Overall, it looks handsome enough and utilitarian, even if all the photos are just renders at this point.

Inside, REO's truck is said to be pretty barebones (it hasn't been revealed yet), but not as barebones as Slate's. There's a digital screen for gauges and one for navigation, but everything else has a physical control. You're probably wondering if it has power or hand-crank windows. REO used its press release to throw a little shade: " Each model will feature power windows for convenience: hand-cranks are best left to others."

The entire idea behind REO is to offer the sort of basic pickups customers loved in the '80s and '90s, trucks that were simple utilitarian workhorses and nothing else. It's an admirable goal, especially given how expensive pickups have become. But do customers want to go backward in powertrain technology, especially with affordable mainstream electric trucks like the Ford Fathom coming to market soon?

So for less money, you can get a potentially more capable truck, in a similar configuration as the Slate, with a bit of extra interior comfort. But you lose the electric powertrain, which reduces maintenance and fuel costs. Instead, you'll have to make due with a generic four-cylinder of some sort. There is one big "if," though. You can only buy one if REO ever actually makes it. Sure, having a design by Frank Stephenson helps it feel more real, but just because they found the money to pay him to design something doesn't mean they'll actually get around to building it.

Slate is already here, it's ready to launch production vehicles soon. We've seen them in person, touched them, and tested them. Until REO can actually muster a viable product, it should probably avoid throwing anymore shade in its press releases.

Disney Touts Its Streaming Success In Latest Earnings

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After a lot of internal upheaval and consolidation, the House of Mouse finds itself in a pretty good place going into its fiscal 2027 year.

So says new CEO Josh D’Amaro and CFO Hugh Johnston during an earnings call on Tuesday. They told investors that Disney exceeded its guidance for the previous quarter and is on track to exceed its three-year adjusted earnings-per-share growth guidance going into the new year.

For the full year, revenue not only increased 8% but broke the $100 billion mark, from $94.billion in the fiscal year 2025 to $102.3 billion in 2026.

Meanwhile, for Disney’s Q4 (the company starts its fiscal year a quarter earlier), revenue increased 16% to $25.9 billion for last quarter, compared to $22.5 the year before.

Within entertainment, advertising grew 10% to $1.7 billion. And SVOD revenue increased 19% to $6.1 billion, with 22% subscription revenue growth.

In TV, D’Amaro highlighted the critical buzz around Hulu’s crime thriller “Furious,” high ratings for the new season of “Dancing with the Stars” and excitement over “The Remarried Empress,” a South Korean romantasy that debuted as the biggest premiere for a local original across Disney+ and Hulu globally. The latter is indicative of Disney’s plan to invest more in international content, a strategy that’s been working wonders for competitors like Netflix.

Sports are also a big deal, of course. Subscription and affiliate fee revenue in the sports segment increased 18% in the quarter to $3.4 billion, with advertising revenue growth of 20%.

Disney will continue to grow its sports footprint next year with expanded college football rights, increased “Monday Night Football” ratings and ESPN’s first ever Super Bowl telecast. (Well, technically it’s ABC’s turn to host the Big Game, but it’ll also be cross-broadcast on other Disney channels.)

Where Disney’s overall streaming strategy is concerned, D’Amaro told investors that there will be a larger focus on “connecting services” – which includes other non-entertainment sides of the business like parks, cruise ships and merchandise.

More practically, though, “connecting services” means finally unifying Disney+ and Hulu into one app, which D’Amaro said would start in December. It also means adding more sports content to Disney+, and introducing creator content from TikTok into the Disney+ mobile app next month.  The move might strike some as strange, but given that ESPN’s short-form Verts product broke records in September and October, maybe there’s something to it.

At the same time, Disney is still exploring the idea of a free streaming product, which leadership first discussed during last quarter’s earnings reports.

Despite – or maybe because of – all the streaming success, Disney announced it will stop reporting its SVOD operating income beginning next quarter. At the same time, the company will also provide additional disclosures related to revenue within the entertainment segment.

SVOD revenues will still be reported, Johnston clarified to investors but so will content and IP revenues. Revenue for linear networks will be combined with the virtual MVPD business.

The goal, said Johnston, is to provide investors with “additional information and a better lens into our content monetization,” while allowing Disney’s team more flexibility to “invest appropriately” in growing SVOD.

In other words, Disney’s likely planning to spend a lot more money on its SVOD business – and maybe it doesn’t want investors to worry too much about how the sausage is getting made.

Disney’s remarkable growth this past quarter isn’t surprising, given how much the company has restructured since D’Amaro took on the CEO role in March. At least 1000 members of Disney’s workforce have been laid off since then, with more job cuts and consolidation reportedly on the way.

All that streamlining is expected to translate into $3 billion in run rate savings by 2029, according to D’Amaro.

To highlight the ways Disney is prioritizing efficiency and consolidation, D’Amaro also shouted out the company’s first ever chief technology officer, Karandeep Anand, who came from the (now bankrupt) chatbot startup Character.ai.

Not surprisingly, Disney plans to continue investing in AI tools and technology across its entire business, even parks. Although D’Amaro didn’t bring up the company’s recent purchase of Character.ai’s assets, he did hype up how the tech is being used in the streaming side of the business, both within Disney+’s content recommendation engine and in the advertising products Disney makes available to its marketing clients.

In addition to Disney’s AI creative studio that uses existing brand material to develop CTV-ready ads, the company also launched an agentic ad campaign tool in August. Both were previewed at its tech and data showcase at CES earlier this year.

Now, almost half of all new ad campaigns are flowing through the agentic tool, according to Disney’s ad sales team.

After selling ads at The Wall Street Journal and WebMD and rising to CRO at The Trade Desk, Jed Dederick is now the first-ever chief revenue officer of creator startup ShopMy.

DecryptAds’ goal, Garcia said, is to give researchers, ad agencies, tech companies and regulators the evidence they need to fix security gaps in the programmatic supply chain.

The mobile growth marketing platform Liftoff reported its second-ever quarterly earnings last week. Shares were up by almost 25% after the company exceeded its revenue target and projected an optimistic profit margin outlook moving forward.

CTV sellers are promising measurable outcomes – even guaranteed store visits – but winning advertiser trust takes more than just measurement.

For the first time, a new feature in Google Ads breaks down the dollar amount a campaign saved from IVT blocking and exactly how much Google is crediting the campaign for bots caught post-billing.

Tim and Chris Vanderhook explain why Viant bought Waymark and how it fits into their ambitious plans for CTV.

AdExchanger is where marketers, agencies, publishers and tech companies go for the latest information on the trends that are transforming digital media and marketing, from data, privacy, identity and AI to commerce, CTV, measurement and mobile.

Bitcoin Lightning developers needed two patches to stop a potentially costly attack

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The initial July update blocked one crash method but missed a persistent vulnerability that could leave affected nodes unable to restart.

Bitcoin's Core Lightning software required two security patches to eliminate a vulnerability that could repeatedly crash payment nodes and potentially expose funds held in other channels.

A Nov. 13 security disclosure revealed that an initial repair released in July failed to address a second attack method that could keep affected nodes offline even after they restarted.

The vulnerability, tracked as LNF-2026-0004, affected Core Lightning, one of the software implementations used to operate nodes on Bitcoin's Lightning Network.

Security researcher Nishant Bansal discovered that attackers could exploit how Core Lightning assigned identifiers to payment channels, creating duplicate records that caused the software to crash.

The weakness originated in the network's channel-opening process, where each payment channel receives an identifier derived from the Bitcoin transaction output that funds it. Core Lightning could accept multiple channels referencing the same funding output, producing identical identifiers that conflicted with assumptions in its internal software.

In the first attack, a malicious peer could create duplicate pending channels and crash a victim's node simply by reconnecting. The attack required no Bitcoin transaction fees because the fraudulent channels did not need on-chain funding. Each subsequent reconnection could trigger another crash, allowing attackers to repeatedly interrupt payment processing.

However, researchers identified a more persistent variation involving previously closed channels.

By reusing an identifier associated with a closed channel and subsequently closing another channel with the same identifier, attackers could trigger a software failure that persisted across restarts.

Unlike the initial attack, this method required on-chain transaction fees but could leave the node unable to recover without a software upgrade.

Core Lightning developers addressed the original vulnerability in version 26.06.6, released on July 22, after researchers privately reported the reconnection attack earlier that month.

The update prevented new channels from reusing funding outputs associated with existing live channels. It also modified connection handling to prevent duplicate records already stored from triggering the original crash.

However, the protection did not extend to identifiers associated with closed channels, leaving the second attack route available.

Researcher Crypt-iQ independently identified the remaining weakness, prompting developers to introduce broader safeguards in version 26.06.8, released Sept. 22.

The second update expanded duplicate-identifier checks across active and closed channels belonging to different peers. It also allowed nodes containing previously stored duplicate records to recover without repeatedly crashing.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

July’s repair checked live channels. September’s also covered closed channels and allowed recovery from duplicates already stored.

The first fix blocked outpoints reused among that peer’s live channels and handled reconnect duplicates. Closed-channel collisions could still cause a persistent restart loop.

The broader fix rejected IDs used by live or closed channels across all peers and let nodes load and close duplicates already stored.

Operators should upgrade to the latest release to prevent collisions and handle stored duplicates.

The closed-channel loop shown is the documented persistent path; a separate free nonpersistent path also existed. This qualitative schematic does not show exploitation, losses or affected-node counts.

Qualitative schematic of the common collision mechanism and two historical patch scopes. Toggle compares July 22 v26.06.6 with September 22 v26.06.8. Connectors show software paths, not counts, timing or money flows.

The closed-channel loop shown is the documented persistent path; a separate free nonpersistent path also existed. This qualitative schematic does not show exploitation, losses or affected-node counts.

The potential financial consequences extended beyond disrupted Lightning payments.

According to the advisory, nodes forced offline could lose the ability to monitor Bitcoin's blockchain for attempts to settle channels using revoked transaction states.

That could allow dishonest counterparties to claim funds using outdated channel balances. Offline nodes could also miss deadlines for collecting time-sensitive payments, placing Bitcoin held in unrelated channels at risk.

The researchers did not report confirmed exploitation or financial losses.

The findings affect Core Lightning versions preceding the complete September repair, with the reconnection vulnerability dating back to version 0.12.0 and the persistent closed-channel crash introduced in version 25.05.

Developers have since released additional security updates, including version 26.06.10, which addresses other vulnerabilities involving channel operations, payment resolution, and remote crashes.

Core Lightning's maintainers have urged operators to upgrade to the latest release to protect their nodes against the disclosed weaknesses.

AI models regularly misidentify civilians as military targets in tests, report found

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Anthropic's Claude Opus 5.5 targeted a school in Iran more than any other model tested, according to evaluator Vals

Vals, an independent AI evaluator, published a report on Tuesday showing that frontier AI models flagged civilians, ambulances and surrendering soldiers as military targets in simulated scenarios.

Vals said no request was blocked by a classifier and that refusals from the models themselves were uncommon. Models that did refuse complied after being told they were playing a game. In a battlefield survey task, most models kept going after seeing casualties from earlier strikes.

The researchers ran three tests: drone target identification and a battlefield survey, both based on the war in Ukraine, and a satellite imagery test of Minab, Iran, where a school sits next to a military complex. The United States bombed the school on Feb. 28, killing over 150 civilians, mostly children, Vals said.

In the satellite test, Anthropic's Claude Opus 5.5 targeted the school more than any other model, Vals said. The model picked the school in 22 of 32 tests, whereas no OpenAI model chose it even once, according to Bloomberg. When the prompt framed the images as coming from a game, the Anthropic model chose the school on 23 occasions, and three OpenAI models chose it on 9, 11 and 13 occasions.

Vals said Claude Opus 4.5, released in November 2025, gave at least one target in 6% of satellite trials, compared with 92% for Opus 5.5. OpenAI models' targeting rate stayed low.

Vals said it did not evaluate targeting accuracy, compliance with the law of armed conflict or performance in real U.S. military workflows. It also said it did not establish how effective the models would be in a deployed weapons system. The firm recommended that AI labs add further protective measures and train their models on alignment.

An Anthropic spokesperson said the report's examples run "very clearly" counter to company policy, and that further safeguards are due to take effect shortly, according to Bloomberg. OpenAI said in a statement shared by Vals that it is working on alignment updates covering drones and their software systems, including assistance framed as simulation, gaming or academic or defensive use.

A Google $GOOGL spokesperson said the company's models were not built with such uses in mind, according to Bloomberg. A Pentagon official said the department declines to weigh in on reports produced by outside parties.

Vals said it disclosed its findings on Nov. 1 to OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Meta $META and SpaceXAI and held publication for two weeks.

The Pentagon designated Anthropic a national security supply chain risk in March, after contract talks collapsed over the company's request for guarantees that Claude would not be used in autonomous lethal weapons systems or for domestic mass surveillance.

In a September report on misuse of its models, Anthropic flagged attempts to enlist Claude in developing software for missiles, armed drones and bombs. The company said it banned the accounts tied to the activity it identified.

Review: Park Hyatt Vienna, My Favorite Hotel In The Austrian Capital

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Park Hyatt Vienna is not only my favorite hotels in Vienna, but one of my favorite Hyatt properties in the world. With excellent service, nice amenities, delicious breakfast, and central location, our latest stay was another great reminder of what a superb property this is.

Although we could have connected directly to Zürich after landing in Vienna, I was able to force a 23.5-hour layover into the itinerary, which gave us a chance to stay to the Park Hyatt Vienna, a hotel I’ve enjoyed many times over the years but it was the first time my children had come along.

I booked this for 35,000 World of Hyatt points (versus a cash rate of over €700) and applied a “Guest of Honor” to the reservation, which essentially marks me as “Globalist” in the system.

Our Austrian flight landed at 11:30 am into VIE and I had emailed the hotel requesting an early check-in, specifically noting that we would be happy to forgo an upgrade in order to access our room a few hours early. Our request was honored.

The Park Hyatt Vienna is ideally located on Am Hof in the heart of the old town, an easy walk from St. Stephen’s Cathedral and many of the places I most enjoy visiting in Vienna.

Checking in, I was welcomed back to the hotel and the amenities were explained, including breakfast and pool hours (most important to my kids!).

We were assigned room 331, a standard room with two twins beds. While not an upgrade, the room was spacious enough and quite comfortable with a desk, round table, chair, and the two beds.

My daughter jumped right into bed to go to sleep (it was, after all, about 3:15 am in Los Angeles), but I pulled her out: no napping today would hopefully mean no jet lag tomorrow.

The bathroom featured really beautiful stonework behind the bathtub and included two sinks, a separate water closet, and walk-in shower.

The room had a wardrobe that included a coffee machine, hot water kettle, and mini-bar including snacks and drinks.

A welcome amenity was also waiting for us including various chocolates and candies.

The room had plenty of 220-volt outlets and also a safe to lock valuables. Unfortunately, the room did not have any USB-C ports or wireless chargers.

A primary reason I booked this hotel was for the pool and wellness. My kids love to swim and I can’t think of a better hotel pool than this one: we spent a lot of time here in the afternoon and evening.

The entire pool and spa area is posh, with a full-range of treatments available but nice say chairs with fresh peaches and plums, a locker room, and a wellness area with a sauna and steam room available to all guests.

Side note: the hotel has a cryogenic machine in the spa that will let you stand in -110ºC temperatures for three minutes…I was tempted and probably would have tried it had I been alone.

Thankfully, there was no drama this time in the sauna!

> Read More: Accused Of Being A Child Predator at Park Hyatt Vienna!

The fitness center is located in the spa area on the lower level, but accessible 24/7 with your room card and includes LifeFitness equipment. I awoke an hour before the kids and went down to workout…a nice way to start the day and fight jet lag.

You mustn’t skip breakfast here, for it is truly a treat.

The Bank is the most beautiful part of the hotel: an ornate dining room that was once the grand lobby when this hotel was still a bank. Breakfast is served from 7:00 am each morning until 10:30 am (11:00 am on weekends) and includes a delicious buffet plus an additional a la carte menu. If breakfast isn’t clouded in your rate, it is €58 each, which while steep is really worthwhile considering all you get with it.

There’s a bar section in The Bank that includes alcohol, including a large cocktail menu, and lighter food. It’s open from 9:00 am to 1:00 am on Monday – Wednesday, till 2:00 am on Thursday – Saturday, and from 5:00 pm to 1:00 pm on Sunday (good luck finding anything open on Sunday in Vienna…).

The hotel’s Viennese café includes cakes, coffee and chocolates, as well as alcoholic drinks and snacks, with an outdoor terrace. Open each day from 9:00 am to 8:00 pm.

Open from 9:00 am to 6:00 pm daily, the Lounge offers a more informal dining experience where you can enjoy hot and cold drinks plus a variety of food. Menu here.

Finally, 24/7 room service is available. Menu here.

There used to be really posh cigar lounge in this hotel, but now it is a beauty salon (Vienna has banned indoor smoking after being a smoker’s haven for so many years).

Our entire say was marked by one pleasant service encounter after another. I had a nice extended conversation with teh doorman and another great conversation with the agent who checked me in. We had a nice chat with our waitress in The Bank and the spa staff was also very friendly. I do speak German, albeit with an American accent, and appreciated that the staff was willing to engage in my less-than-perfect German as opposed to switching to English.

This was another wonderful stay at the Park Hyatt Vienna and I’ll sum it up by saying simply that I cannot wait to return.

My original review is below, dating all the way back to August 2014. Heidi and I went for my birthday just weeks after we got married…the hotel had recently opened and we had a great stay. It’s nice to see this hotel age so gracefully and what a joy it is to share this property and the lovely city of Vienna with my children.

Vienna is a stately city rich with majestic architecture, an erudite culture, and a regal sense of style. In the center of the old city, replete with cobblestone walkways, horse drawn carriages, and glistening statues of historical luminaries, a new Park Hyatt hotel has opened on the site of a former monarchical bank. My wife and I celebrated my birthday in Vienna last weekend and stayed for two nights at Park Hyatt’s newest property.

A historic property located on Am Hof 2 in the heart of the First District of Vienna, a UNESCO World Heritage site, the hotel’s location cannot be beat. Within a few paces is the Goldenes Quartier shopping district, St. Stephan’s Cathedral, Hofburg Palace, Spanish Riding School, and many museums.

We began the day in Frankfurt with an early flight on Austrian Airlines to Vienna. I sent a note to the main hotel e-mail, park.vienna@hyatt.com, just before takeoff requesting an early check-in and had a response waiting when I landed in Vienna – the room was ready.

From Vienna Airport you have two public transportation options for getting into the city – the City Airport Train (CAT) which travels non-stop to the heart of Vienna or a suburban train (S-Bahn) which calls at several stations on the way into town, taking about 15 minutes longer than the direct service. The CAT is 14EUR one-way while the S-Bahn is 4.40EUR. We took the S-Bahn into town, then connected on the underground train (U-Bahn) to Herrengasse Station, which is just a short walk from the hotel.

A smiling porter greeted us outside the door and took our luggage. Inside, a friendly young man checked us in, speaking first in English when he noticed my American passport but then switching to German upon the request of my wife that I practice my Deutsch! He escorted us upstairs and demonstrated the hi-tech features of our room. Like many Park Hyatt properties, lighting and draperies are electronically controlled by a central push-button unit.

I loved the room – a spacious standard room with tremendous natural light, a great view, and functionality. The room featured copious electrical outlets – an essential amenity when taking my business on the road, as I did here even on my birthday weekend. The layout of the couch in front of the bed in front of the desk nicely utilized the space in the room and the separate entry hall insulated the room from outside noise.

Two doors decorated in geometric art-deco style, closed off the bathroom and entry hall from the bedroom. The bathroom was very large (and well-placed mirrors made it appear even larger) and featured a sizeable bathtub, walk-in rain shower, two sinks, and a separate water closet.

A minibar featured an Illy coffee machine with several packets of complimentary espresso and snack and drink for purchase. The refrigerator did make a rather loud humming sound that could be heard even when the bedroom door was closed. I am a heavy sleeper, but my wife woke me up one night to “turn the climate off” yet it was not the air conditioning, it was the refrigerator! It was not terrible, just noticeable.

It was warm outside and we had a problem getting the air conditioning to activate. Turns out one of the windows was open just ajar – just make sure all your windows are tightly shut and your room key is in the slot by the door or else your air conditioning will not function. Once on, the room cooled down rapidly. Vienna boasts exceptional weather so most of the year there is no need for a/c anyway.

The hotel offers three dining options in addition to 24-hour room service; The Bank, Pearl, and The Lobby Lounge. Pearl bar features an outdoor terrace in addition to a contemporary indoor seating area and specializes in cocktails. A tapas menu is available with food as substantial as Wiener Schnitzel also available.

The Lobby Lounge offers drinks and light meals and sits next to the Living Room, a cigar and whiskey lounge that is one of the hotel’s best features. Admittedly, I see little value in setting fire to and then inhaling a plant that is linked to lung cancer but many of my friends and my brother hold a different opinion, so I find myself in cigar bars quite often. If you are a conneousier of cigars or just enjoy an opulent room to enjoy a drink in with friends, this bar is not to be missed. With plush couches and rich wooden paneling on the walls, I felt like I was part of the gentry mulling over world affairs in the cigar and brandy room onboard the Titanic.

Finally, The Bank is the hotel’s most opulent restaurant and central to the character of the property. Here, on the site of the former cashier’s hall, we had breakfast each morning. Breakfast was 33EUR and the buffet was expansive with all that you would expect in a comprehensive breakfast—fresh squeezed juices, cereals, fruits, breads, cold-cuts, cheeses, breakfast meats, and eggs. Coffee (it was tremendous) as well as pancakes, waffles, French Toast, and eggs are included in the buffet and can be ordered from your server.

We spent hours in here each morning enjoying a leisurely breakfast (breakfast ends at 10:30a during the week and 11:00a on weekends) and found the service to be warm, personal, and punctual. I thought everything was great – my wife had a problem with her eggs on the first morning (soft boiled eggs came out hard-boiled, then an omelet ordered with no cheese was delivered with cheese) but the issues were quickly remedied and did not reoccur the next morning. The juices were remarkably tasty (apple, orange, and even grapefruit were exceptional) and my favorite part of the breakfast was the cheese-covered cooked tomatoes.

Lunch and dinner are also offered each night in The Bank and you will find no menu posted online because the menu, sourced with local ingredients, changes often. Prices were moderate and I was prepared to dine at the restaurant for my birthday, but when mentioning that I was especially looking for fish, the concierge directed me to a restaurant called Kornat about 15 minutes away by foot, which specializes in fish and seafood.

So I missed trying out The Bank for an evening meal, but I did appreciate the restaurant recommendation for the food was amazing and we enjoyed a prime outdoor table thanks to a call ahead from the concierge.

I do have one gripe and only one true gripe about the property: the in-room wireless internet speed was unreliable. The Park Hyatt Vienna offers complimentary wired and wi-fi internet, which is a nice feature, but I found myself on multiple occasions banging my hand on the desk with disgust as poor connectivity led to unacceptably long page load times and the inability to make internet-based telephone calls. Other times, though, the interent worked perfectly well with pages loading fast and no issue with Skype calls or viewing YouTube videos. Admittedly, I did not not bring my ethernet cable converter for my MacBook Air so I was unable to test the wired internet speed.

A large fitness studio with state-of-the-art, brand new equipment was much appreciated. Several bikes, treadmills, and ellipticals filled one side of the room while strength training equipment and free weights filled out the other. The room itself, with brass candelabras and wooden floors was an elegant place to work out and apples, bottled water, refrigerated towels, newspapers, and magazines were available.

The gym overlooks the swimming pool below, which sits on the site of the former bank vault. With an ornate frieze relief and elongated white leather couches, it offers a refreshing antidote to the hot afternoon sun (or cold). Adjacent to the pool is a lounge with chairs to relax and also steam room and sauna facilities. My one regret is that we did not spend an extra day in Vienna just to lounge around the spa. Certainly, seeing Vienna is the priority if your time is limited, but this is a great complimentary benefit of the hotel that seems to go unutilized, as I saw many hotel guests and no one using the pool or spa lounge. Full spa services are available here in the Arany Spa and you can even see the original 3-ton bank vault door.

This hotel is a category six property, meaning it is 25,000 Hyatt Gold Passport points per night or 12,500 points plus a $150 co-pay (my preferred method of payment to help stretch out my points). The daily rate during the summer is about 375EUR or $500. Gold Passport availblity is good.

I can’t tell you how much I enjoyed the city of Vienna and both my wife (who is not usually a city person) and I wish to return soon. When we do, we will definitely choose the Park Hyatt again, allowing an extra day for the spa and enjoying at a more leisurely pace one of Europe’s greatest capital cities.

EU, ASEAN pursue deeper trade ties, but hurdles remain as businesses struggle with informal barriers

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

2027 could be a landmark year for EU–Southeast Asia trade, with several deals nearing completion. But the harder task will be to ensure that businesses actually use them.

The European Union and the Association of Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) could be heading for a breakthrough year in 2027, when the two sides mark 50 years of diplomatic ties.

A long-awaited trade agreement with Indonesia is expected to take effect, while a deal with the Philippines is nearing completion and negotiations with Thailand and Malaysia are making progress. By the end of next year, the EU could have trade deals in place with six Southeast Asian states, up from just two today.

The anniversary could also revive the long-standing ambition of a broader trade agreement between the EU and ASEAN.

On November 17, the European Commission hosted a dialogue in Brussels to discuss trade relations with Southeast Asia, including progress on existing and pending free trade agreements (FTAs).

But attention is turning to what comes next. "Finalizing FTAs is only the beginning, not the end," Chris Humphrey, executive director of the EU-ASEAN Business Council, told DW.

FTAs can reduce tariffs and remove trade restrictions, but that is of little use if businesses don't know how to use them, he added.

"Unfortunately, in many cases, this is where there is a failure. Communication and training are key," Humphrey said.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

For Brussels, the priority should now be to tackle the less conspicuous obstacles to trade, he stressed.

Cutting customs delays, simplifying regulations, and helping smaller European businesses enter unfamiliar Southeast Asian markets will determine whether an FTA succeeds.

"European companies with little or no experience in Southeast Asia would benefit from greater on-the-ground support, particularly practical, firsthand guidance on informal business practices and the unwritten local rules," Alfred Gerstl, an associate professor at Palacky University Olomouc in the Czech Republic, told DW.

One option is to expand the assistance offered by European chambers of commerce, trade delegations, and EU offices in Southeast Asia. This could range from helping firms find distributors to explaining local licensing requirements and how to qualify for preferential tariffs.

In Vietnam, for instance, sustained pressure from Brussels has helped improve market access for European pharmaceutical firms. In Indonesia, the EU has worked on trade facilitation while pressing Jakarta over import restrictions and halal certification requirements, said Camille Nessel, a researcher at the Universite Libre de Bruxelles.

Regular consultations with businesses on both sides could also help Brussels spot problems before they become trade disputes.

Trade policy has also acquired a geopolitical purpose in Brussels. "If trade policy is expected to reduce strategic dependencies, we are asking it to deliver on objectives that remain insufficiently defined, let alone demonstrating how to achieve them," Nessel told DW.

The EU wants to reduce its dependence on a handful of suppliers for the minerals needed for batteries and other clean technologies, while Southeast Asian producers, particularly Indonesia, want foreign investors to help develop domestic processing industries rather than merely buy raw materials.

The EU and Indonesia have already clashed over Jakarta's restrictions on nickel exports.

European investment in processing facilities, agreements on environmental standards, and greater regulatory certainty would give both sides concrete gains, said Nessel. "The real value of an FTA is not just opening a market but giving the EU the leverage to keep it open," she added.

Pasha Hsieh, Lee Kong Chian Professor of Law and Jean Monnet Chair at Singapore Management University, said Brussels should prioritize ratifying its investment protection agreements with Singapore and Vietnam.

Signed in 2018 and 2019, respectively, the agreements still require ratification by all EU member states before entering into force. "These delays underline the need to follow through on negotiated commitments," Hsieh said.

The issue is particularly relevant as the EU looks to develop investment protocols in other Southeast Asian states where investors have sometimes faced uncertainty over legal protections. Indonesia, for instance, has terminated numerous bilateral investment treaties, including agreements with the Netherlands and Spain.

A larger question is whether the EU should continue pursuing bilateral deals with Southeast Asian countries or revive its original ambition of a region-to-region agreement with ASEAN.

The EU and ASEAN launched negotiations for a region-wide FTA in 2007 but suspended them two years later amid disagreements over wide economic disparities among ASEAN members and political concerns. Technical discussions resumed in 2017, but little has come of them.

ASEAN already has regional agreements with China and several other economies. Beijing upgraded its free trade agreement with ASEAN in October 2025.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Region-to-region frameworks extend beyond trade. Humphrey, of the EU-ASEAN Business Council, called for negotiations to begin on a region-to-region Digital Trade Agreement and Investment Protection Agreement.

The EU-Singapore Digital Trade Agreement, which entered into force in February 2026, offers a possible model for wider cooperation, he noted, adding that common approaches to customs procedures, supply-chain standards, and green technologies could create greater alignment with ASEAN members that are not yet ready for full FTAs.

"European businesses have long complained of operating at a disadvantage due to the lack of a region-to-region deal. It is high time that both sides elevated their ambitions and embraced the idea of working towards such an agreement," Humphrey said.

The 2027 anniversary would be a convenient deadline for agreeing on what comes next, but Gerstl warned against mistaking another round of diplomatic ceremony for progress.

"There is a risk that attention will focus almost exclusively on the 2027 summit in Singapore and that the group photo will become the defining image of the event, only for momentum to fade in the years that follow," he added.

Tech company Huawei built its business on repeated crimes, prosecutors tell jurors

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Federal prosecutors urged jurors Monday to convict Chinese technology company Huawei, arguing that the company had grown by repeatedly committing crimes. (Photo from Shutterstock)

Federal prosecutors urged jurors Monday to convict Chinese technology company Huawei, arguing during closing statements in the ongoing 11-week trial involving trade secrets theft and sanctions evasion that the company had grown by repeatedly committing crimes.

According to a Monday story from Law.com, Meredith Arfa, the assistant U.S. attorney for the Eastern District of New York, told jurors that Huawei spent two decades pursuing growth “by any means possible.”

To illustrate the trade secrets allegations, Arfa played a video of a former Huawei employee removing part of Tappy, T-Mobile’s robot for testing phone touchscreens, from the carrier’s lab. She also showed side-by-side images of Cisco’s and Huawei’s router source code, saying the two matched exactly—down to an engineer’s stray comment mentioning someone named “Bob.”

“This simply couldn’t happen without copying,” Arfa said in her summation.

She further alleged that Huawei managers encouraged theft by paying bonuses for obtaining confidential competitor information, and that employees boasted about it on an internal forum.

On the sanctions compliance allegations, prosecutors said Huawei secretly controlled Skycom, a subsidiary that supplied sensitive technology to Iran’s government. Arfa said the company repeatedly lied about owning Skycom. She also pointed to admissions by Meng Wanzhou, Huawei’s chief financial officer, who signed a deferred prosecution agreement after spending three years under house arrest. Meng acknowledged that Skycom’s operations violated U.S. sanctions, and that she misled HSBC, Huawei’s primary bank, about its compliance, Arfa noted.

Huawei, one of the largest telecommunications companies in the world, denies the allegations, contending that the case rests on the independent actions of rogue employees who were disciplined.

The government announced the case in 2019. The company is represented by Jenner & Block, Sidley Austin and Steptoe.

Most people don't associate the original Resident Evil with being overly grisly, but a lot's changed in the 30 years since it released. These days, we've become desensitized by dismemberment as a technical showcase rather than something to be horrified by, making 1996's Resident Evil look downright cozy next to today's horror pantheon. And yet, it was apparently shocking enough 30 years ago that series creator Shinji Mikami worried it wouldn't be fit for sale until Sony stepped in with some clutch packaging additions.

Mikami appeared on a Japanese podcast (timestamp) last month to talk about Resident Evil's legacy amid its 30th anniversary, but his comments haven't been widely reported until this week when Twitter user Genki started spotlighting the highlights. In one such example, Mikami tells the interviewers that OG Resident Evil was considered so "grotesque" that Capcom feared it wouldn't be fit for sale. We can only go off machine translation here as there's no English transcription, but Mikami says it was Sony that stepped in and saved the day by adding stickers to Resident Evil's packaging warning potential buyers of the game's gory nature.

Resident Evil creator Shinji Mikami says they were worried that the original Resident Evil game would not be allowed to go on sale because it was too grotesque but Sony had a solution that saved them. He said they didn't know what to do but Sony said that if they put a sticker on the game warning about the grotesque scenes then it would be okay to be released. Mikami would continue to make excessively bloody games even beyond his time at Capcom with The Evil Within 1 and 2 at Tango Gameworks, but it's unclear if his next game has anything to do with the survival horror genre. He said in the same interview that his new studio Unbound is on track to release its debut title in 2027, but previous comments indicate he might be wanting to explore different genres after spending three decades scaring the bejesus out of players.

Does It Matter That We’ve Taken the “Mormon” Out of the Mormon Wives?

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There is something about hearing “come ye saints, come ye sinners” that sets off a Pavlovian response in my brain; a physical trigger to sling the (dirty) Diet Coke back and turn the volume up. It means The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives is back, this time with its OC-set spinoff: The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County.

In the show’s marketing, Hulu leans into the California setting as the clear divide between SLOMW: OC and its Utah predecessor. SLOMW: OC isn’t as ensconced in the shadow of the Mormon church as the Utah show is. Instead, it offers a more laid-back version of Mormonism (they drink! they get tattoos! they’re in throuples!). But in taking the “Mormon” out of the “Mormon Wives,” are we missing out on what makes the show so interesting?

It’s a bold choice to greenlight a spinoff about Mormons without actually casting any Mormons. In fact, of the eight new cast members (Jen Affleck is the only familiar face), only four identify as practicing Mormons at all: Mayci Neely’s identical-looking sister McCall DaPron, McCall’s bestie Jordan McClanahan, YouTuber Madison Bontempo, and Ashleigh Pease.

Meanwhile, the other half of the cast have either left the church altogether, as is the case with influencers Aspyn Ovard and Salome Andea, or were never religious to begin with like podcasters Avery Woods and Bobbi Althoff. It’s the “Molly Mormons,” or traditional Mormons, vs. the “Black Cats,” the rebels of the group. What connects these women is not so much a shared religion as a shared career path: influencing and podcasting.

SLOMW Utah’s strength was its portrayal of the contradictory rules of Mormonism. For example, what do you mean it goes against the church to drink caffeine but it’s totally fine to get high off laughing gas at a Botox appointment?? The rules only got more interesting as the show progressed and the women went from housewives to reality TV megastars overnight. Suddenly they were grappling with bigger religious conundrums: how to be both subordinate in the church and a breadwinner at home.

SLOMW: OC takes a different approach by focusing more on influencer mom drama, shifting away from the central tension of the show: women striving for independence and equality while navigating deeply patriarchal relationships. Take Mayci in season 5 when she fends off a fourth pregnancy request from her mother, sister, and husband (keep in mind, she just popped out baby #3!). Or Jessi, whose husband Jordan felt emasculated by the number of zeros in her paycheck.

In the Orange County-set spinoff, the men are curiously absent. Madison, Salome, and Aspyn are the only women to feature their romantic partners on screen—and even then it’s only a few moments of the show’s eight-episode run. The absence of the men feels like a strategic response to the DadTok hate that plagued the Utah show. But Hulu may have mistaken our TFP relationship fatigue for not wanting to see messy relationships at all.

Instead the show has channeled all that messy relationship tension into the women’s dynamics with each other. Their fights originate from the different ways they interpret feminism, religion, money, and power. Take McCall and Bobbi, for instance. McCall, who sees the group’s religious divide as her “McCalling” to convert, is the clear villain of season 1 because she tries to center Mormonism in the show. It’s Bobbi, hoodie pulled up and strings pulled tight, who brings McCall back down to earth and challenges her to see her faith through different perspectives.

“Show me the [bible] verse that says to be a bitch and talk shit on people, please,” Bobbi throws out in episode 4 after learning McCall made a dig about her comedy show and threatened to have another cast member replaced for not piling on Bobbi too. Later in the season, Bobbi confronts McCall over her drama with Aspyn Ovard and urges her to rethink her mean girl internet campaign. She points out that McCall might not agree with all of Aspyn’s life decisions, but Aspyn has a terminally ill child; McCall should be the bigger, more religious woman and show her some grace.

If the show wants to lean away from messy marriages, then it needs to lean into the messiness of religion in friendships. What are the boundaries that divide? What are the boundaries that unite? People hate McCall but, sorry, she is very good mess! Watching her try (and fail) to convert Bobbi into wearing pastels and saying a singular nice word about the church is the most fun I’ve had in a while. Without the religious tension, the show becomes hot, rich women fighting in public, and there are stronger contenders in that genre (hello, Real Housewives).

Maybe taking the “Mormon” out of “Mormon Wives” will rejuvenate a flailing brand, but the religious tension has to stay a priority regardless of the women’s religious affiliations. Taking the “Mormon” out of “Mormon Wives” is bad for the show—and bad for the mess!

You Can Get This Micro-E-Reader for Under $60 Before Black Friday

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Black Friday is usually a great time to grab a new e-reader, but the ongoing RAM crisis has made that a question mark this year: Amazon recently raised the prices of all its Kindles by around $50 and refreshed the entire lineup, which means your odds of grabbing one at a big discount are low. But you aren't totally out of luck: If you haven't noticed, micro-e-readers—I'm talking credit card-sized, with screens that measure around four inches—are all the rage in 2026, and right now, you can pick up one of them for less than $60.

If you too can see the appeal of carrying all of your books with you on a device so small you'll forget it's even in your pocket, it's a great time to jump in: The recently released Xteink X4 Classic is currently 34% off in an early Black Friday sale, but you'll need to act quickly: The discount will soon be gone.

Xteink is a Chinese startup that shook up the e-reading world last year with the launch of the X4, a super bare-bones device that made up for its lack of features and usability with a cheap sub-$70 price point. Since then, a huge online community of fans has formed, sharing tips on Reddit and even coming together to crowdsource an alternate operating system that fixed a lot of early problems. Xteink quickly followed up the X4 with a slate of other devices, including the X3 and the X4 Pro, which adds a front light and touchscreen (at the cost of screen clarity and two physical buttons).

The X4 Classic was released in September, improving on the original X4 with quality-of-life upgrades like a larger battery, relocated page-turn buttons, a real-time clock, and a tilt-to-turn-page feature. It's also 6g lighter and a millimeter thinner than the X4, though there is a trade-off involved there: It drops the USB-C charging port in favor of magnetic pogo pins that require a separate adapter (included).

These upgrades also increased the cost: The X4 Classic typically retails for $89, $20 more than the discontinued X4 and only $10 less than the X4 Pro. But right now, the X4 Classic is $59, or around 34% off, and its lowest price ever. It's a good option for those who prioritize crisp, readable text above the conveniences of a built-in light or touchscreen, as those add extra layers between the inner screen and outer glass, and provided you understand what you're getting into: These devices support only e-books without Digital Rights Management (DRM), which means you can't use it to read your existing library of Kindle books. On the plus side, there's no longer a need to worry about flashing new firmware if you don't want to, as the native version has been totally overhauled and is now one of your best options.

In short, yes, Black Friday still offers discounts that can be rare throughout the rest of the year. If there’s something you want to buy, or you’re shopping for gifts, it’s a good time to look for discounts on what you need, especially tech sales, home improvement supplies, and fitness tech. Of course, if you need to save money, the best way to save is not to buy anything.

Follow our Black Friday coverage for the latest and best deals our team finds. Another good tip is to only buy what you need. It sounds simple, but making a list of the things you want before jumping into a sale with millions of products helps you focus and make smarter decisions.

No. If you find the product you're looking for with a major discount right now, get it. It's not likely to drop any lower, but it could always run out of stock. Take advantage of price matching or guarantees instead of waiting. Remember, you can always return products later if you change your mind. For the majority of products, the deal from Black Friday will be the same one on Cyber Monday.

Cerebras Puts Disaggregated Inference Into Production With GPUs

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Cerebras Systems reported on November 17, 2026, that its production disaggregated inference deployment, which assigns prompt processing to AMD Instinct GPUs and response generation to CS-3 systems, delivered five times the throughput per megawatt of a CS-3-only configuration without reducing token-generation speed.

The result applies to Cerebras Inference Cloud. The measurement came from a shared GPT-OSS-120B endpoint on November 14, 2026, comparing throughput per megawatt before and after disaggregation was enabled and normalizing the aggregated configuration to a 1× baseline. Shifting prompt work to the GPUs leaves the CS-3 systems’ serving capacity available for generating responses, expanding the fast-inference capacity Cerebras can offer through its cloud.

The production disclosure follows Cerebras’ October 1, 2026 explanation of disaggregated inference, which reported a 5× gain in early tests with the same wafer-scale-engine footprint. The new announcement moves beyond that early result by supplying a production measurement and a deployment package for infrastructure partners.

Each inference request has two principal stages. Prefill processes the input prompt and creates intermediate model state; decode uses that state to produce output tokens one at a time, feeding each new token back into the model. Because every decode step reads model data from memory again, memory bandwidth is central to interactive generation speed. Prefill instead performs more computation across the prompt in parallel, making it a better match for accelerators suited to that compute load.

Cerebras’ wafer-scale systems place model data in on-chip SRAM rather than relying primarily on off-chip high-bandwidth memory. Cerebras describes its wafer-scale chip as carrying tens of gigabytes of SRAM and tens of petabytes per second of memory bandwidth—350× the SRAM capacity and 5,400× the memory bandwidth of a typical GB300 GPU. That capacity allows more concurrent requests and model layers to remain directly on the chip for rapid decode.

The economic target is more customer-serving capacity and revenue potential from the same power budget in power-constrained data centers. Cerebras cites Muse, Copilot and Codex as examples of rapidly scaling agentic applications that require highly interactive responses, so the capacity increase must preserve generation speed to be useful for those workloads.

Infrastructure partners can use a Cerebras-provided reference architecture to co-locate their chosen prefill accelerators and Cerebras systems on a unified network. They then have two integration routes: an end-to-end software stack intended to shorten time to market, or a decode-only SDK for partners that want more control.

The full stack routes requests between prefill and decode pools and manages replica capacity and health. Its prefill engine can run open-source inference engines such as vLLM or SGLang, while Cerebras supplies the decode engine. KV Portal transfers intermediate model state between the two engines over RDMA, including the key-value caches that preserve request-specific context after prefill.

With the decode-only option, partners retain their own inference framework and prefill engine while using Cerebras’ decode engine and KV Portal. The SDK also includes a mock decode replica and a reference integration with an open-source disaggregated inference framework and prefill engine. Amazon Web Services, Fireworks AI, Gimlet Labs and General Compute are named as early-access partners, and Cerebras is expanding access to additional partners.

Cerebras plans to deploy disaggregated inference broadly across hundreds of megawatts in its own inference cloud and partner deployments by the end of 2027. That is a deployment target, not capacity already operating.

The announcement therefore combines a measured cloud configuration with a software path for outside operators. The central scaling question is whether the throughput advantage measured on one shared endpoint can be reproduced across partners’ chosen accelerators, frameworks and deployment sizes as Cerebras pursues that broader rollout.

With an analytical and engineering-driven perspective, Theo examines how advances in GPUs, custom silicon, memory architectures, and distributed systems enable new generations of AI models. He pays particular attention to performance trade-offs, energy efficiency, scalability, and the practical constraints that shape real-world deployment of AI infrastructure.

Vertiv Expands 800 VDC Portfolio for AI Compute Pods and Data Halls

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Vertiv on November 17, 2026 introduced the PowerDirect 9000 and PowerNexus MV SST, extending 800-volt direct-current (800 VDC) power beyond individual racks to AI compute pods and data halls. The PowerNexus is a 5 MW solid-state transformer that converts medium-voltage alternating current (AC) to 800 VDC in one stage.

The additions respond to rising AI power requirements by giving operators three places to set the AC-to-DC conversion boundary: beside an IT rack, ahead of a pod, or at the data hall’s medium-voltage connection. Vertiv expects AC and DC architectures to coexist, with no single adoption timetable. The accompanying architecture portfolio combines conversion, storage, distribution, protection, controls and monitoring, allowing 800 VDC to be introduced without committing an entire facility to one design.

The rack-level option remains the PowerDirect 5000, introduced in May 2026 for deployments that retain upstream AC infrastructure. It sits beside an IT rack and accepts 480 VAC, with 800 VDC or ±400 VDC output; Vertiv’s rating puts it at up to 800 kW, with battery backup designed for at least 50 seconds of hold-up. Moving conversion and storage outside the IT rack frees 8 to 16 rack units for compute, while using one power rack for one or two IT racks helps isolate faults and simplify maintenance.

Inside the compute rack, the 1U PowerDirect 3000 shelf converts 800 VDC to 54 VDC for compute trays. The 90 kW unit delivers up to 98.5% peak efficiency and 180% pulse power for 0.5 milliseconds in Vertiv’s specifications. The same shelf connects to the pod-level design, creating a common final conversion stage at the rack.

For a row or pod, the new PowerDirect 9000 consolidates conversion into one system ahead of the racks. The power center converts 480 VAC to 800 VDC, is rated by Vertiv at 1.5 MW and scales in 60 kW increments through 27 hot-pluggable power-supply units. PowerBar Track DC busway carries power overhead to the shelves, with a metered, breaker-protected drop at every rack.

EnergyCore Li5 battery cabinets connect directly to the DC bus. Each cabinet is rated by Vertiv at 200 kW of continuous backup and up to 98% conversion efficiency, with as many as six operating in parallel. Consolidating conversion, backup and distribution removes the need for a power rack beside every IT rack, recovers row space and supports mixed AC and DC loads, including colocation sites with different tenants, workloads and GPU generations.

At data-hall level, the PowerNexus’s outdoor, liquid-cooled container is rated by Vertiv at up to 98% AC-to-DC efficiency. It eliminates two to three intermediate conversion steps in conventional power trains, provides galvanic isolation between input and output, and can support a 4.8 MW super-pod reference architecture without chaining. Moving conversion outdoors can recover indoor space for compute, while fewer stages help reduce cumulative electrical losses.

When paired with external energy storage through a DC/DC converter on the 800 VDC bus, the system can ride through electrical faults and smooth AI workload fluctuations, according to Vertiv. Bidirectional power flow can improve grid power quality and provide active power support, while battery communication ports coordinate storage with rapid load changes. The containerized design also supports faster deployment and standardized replication across campuses.

Safety features across the portfolio include touch-proof busbars, high-voltage isolation, midpoint grounding, ground-fault detection and arc-flash prevention. The accompanying service offerings are purpose-built for DC operating environments, rather than adapted from legacy AC procedures, according to Vertiv.

Vertiv is showcasing the portfolio at SC26 from November 17 to 22, 2026, with pilot programs, technical validation and customer-specific collaborative development underway. These options let operators match the conversion boundary to the facility: retain AC infrastructure during a rack retrofit, consolidate power around a pod, or move medium-voltage conversion outdoors for a campus. The choice extends beyond voltage, because each architecture also determines where backup, fault isolation and conversion losses are managed.

Kyndryl’s Boulder Site Welcomes Argentum AI as Flagship Anchor Tenant

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Argentum AI and Kyndryl announced a 20-year, $630 million data center collaboration on November 17, 2026, covering an initial 10 megawatts of capacity at Kyndryl’s Boulder, Colorado, facility. Argentum is deploying NVIDIA B300 systems there for AI inference.

Deployment is already underway in a fully Tier III environment. The companies say the enterprise-grade infrastructure is intended to run demanding AI applications at scale and to give Argentum’s customers a faster path to high-performance inference capacity. The existing facility was built for the power density of current-generation GPU systems, aligning the site with the hardware Argentum is installing.

Under the agreement, Argentum becomes the facility’s flagship anchor tenant and gains access to Kyndryl’s data center capacity. “Our agreement gives us a unique opportunity to put Kyndryl capacity to work in an innovative way,” said Jamie Rutledge, president of Kyndryl U.S. He said the company’s mission-critical capabilities and decades of operational and services experience would support Argentum as it creates capacity for customers.

Argentum originates, finances and operates GPU clusters, delivering dedicated bare-metal GPU capacity to AI model builders, inference providers and enterprises. Its deployment model secures site control and power before GPU procurement, then coordinates financing, original equipment manufacturer relationships, cluster design and operational partners. The company says this approach is designed around customer demand, with capacity matched to workload requirements.

The Kyndryl agreement supplies the facility and power-capacity side of that model, while Argentum coordinates the GPU deployment and customer-facing service. It also illustrates why existing high-density sites matter to the company’s approach: Argentum promotes power-first sourcing as a faster alternative to construction-dependent buildouts.

Argentum’s model also addresses how those deployments are financed. It describes financing structures designed for contracted AI deployment, intended to scale capacity without waiting for capital to be assembled deal by deal. The long-duration Kyndryl commitment fits that approach by pairing a defined infrastructure requirement with Argentum’s broader financing and cluster-operation role.

Customers can choose how much of that stack Argentum manages. Hardware-as-a-Service provides physical infrastructure, power and connectivity while the customer runs the operating system, software and operations. Bare-Metal-as-a-Service adds operating-system delivery, break-fix work and maintenance, while Platform-as-a-Service extends Argentum’s responsibility through AI application management and reporting.

On its official website, Argentum reports 77,000 GPUs contracted for deployment, 344 megawatts of contracted power and 13 global data center sites under exclusivity. Those company-wide figures show a much wider infrastructure pipeline; they are not measures of the Kyndryl site itself.

The companies intend to broaden their collaboration as Argentum’s enterprise AI infrastructure needs evolve. Boulder is the immediate test of that intention: an active GPU deployment that could anchor a broader infrastructure relationship if the companies turn their expansion plans into further commitments.

HeyGen Launches Avatar 6 with Script-Aware Gestures and 2K Video

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

HeyGen introduced Avatar 6, its latest audio-driven avatar model, in a technical report published November 17, 2026. The model generates video of a specific person from a single photograph, with an optional short identity recording, and HeyGen reports native 2K output, no fixed duration limit and availability in its web app, mobile app and API from mid-November 2026.

Avatar 6 is designed to reproduce a person’s appearance, manner of speaking and habits of motion together. Its in-house system combines the generator, a super-resolution stage that restores details such as skin texture and hair, a motion planner and HeyGen Voice for speech in the speaker’s own voice. Customers can choose Avatar 6 Quality or Avatar 6 Turbo, the latter using HeyGen’s accelerated inference stack for lower latency.

On the product page, HeyGen says Avatar 6 uses a script’s meaning to decide where pointing, counting or directional movement fits. Users can separately specify mood, facial expression or hand movement for finer control. Written motion prompts can attach an action to a particular phrase, such as waving during a greeting or counting on the fingers through a list. The motion planner times those actions to the relevant words while preserving the subject’s characteristic movement between them.

HeyGen’s demonstrations include continuous videos lasting 25 to 28 minutes, each generated from one audio track without loops, so the running time follows the speech. The same framework also produces two speakers in one frame; one demonstration is a two-minute host-and-guest podcast that alternates wide shots with closer views of each person.

Avatar 6’s recorded-video editor can replace words or add a gesture while regenerating only the edited span, with new speech synthesized in the person’s voice. In HeyGen’s 20-edit comparison, 34.4% of frames changed on average, while MiniMax H3-max, Wan 3.0, Seedance 2.5 and HappyHorse Edit altered 99.7% to 100%. That preservation of the surrounding take is useful for correcting a finished presenter recording without recreating the entire shot.

The 84.4% indistinguishability score reported by HeyGen has a specific meaning: external raters selected Avatar 6 as the real recording in 42.2% of 270 side-by-side answers, and the score doubles that selection rate because 50% would represent chance. The side-by-side test involved 20 external raters and 76 people. In separate single-clip tests, 65.1% of answers judged Avatar 6 clips using the person’s own voice to be real, against 78.9% for genuine recordings.

HeyGen’s own external-rater comparisons were narrower against some general video models: among answers with a preference, Avatar 6 won 50.1% against Wan 3.0 from a photograph, described as a tie, 54.9% against Kling 4.0 from footage and 72.4% against Seedance 2.5 from footage.

New Measure’s early Avatar Arena results ranked Avatar 6 first in both categories, with Elo-scale preference scores of 1,197 for personal avatars and 1,237 for stock presenters; each category has an average score of 1,000. Creators compared two videos reading the same script with the provider names hidden and video order randomized. The counted votes and edits ran from October 27, 2026, through November 16, 2026.

New Measure cautions that participants chose to use the arena, the typical script was 62 words and both videos lasted 35 seconds or less in 97% of completed comparisons. The results do not establish performance on long videos or in languages with insufficient testing. Preference also did not mean faster delivery: HeyGen personal-avatar videos typically took about three minutes, versus about one minute for Runway GWM-1 Avatars, and one in ten HeyGen personal-avatar videos took more than nine and a half minutes. The timings exclude avatar setup and speech preparation.

The API documentation lists heygen-avatar-6 and heygen-avatar-6-turbo, with 1080p as the default resolution. A Model API request combines a reference image or video with speech audio, while the Application API starts from a HeyGen avatar look and a script with voice or audio. The Application API supports Avatar 6 only for single-avatar requests, excluding raw-image jobs, studio scenes, batch creation and scene editing; omitting the engine selection continues to use Avatar IV.

Consent rules depend on the input. Before a footage-based HeyGen avatar can render, the person must record an on-camera consent statement containing a session-specific one-time code, and HeyGen verifies that the consent speaker matches the training-footage subject. Photo references are available to all API workspaces with the person’s permission, whereas realistic video references are enterprise-only. The documentation says video-reference consent is verified before submission, although some contracts can operate without that step after case-by-case review.

Avatar 6 gives production users more control over identity, motion and revision, with long continuous output extending beyond short presenter clips. The independent arena supports its short-clip appeal, but leaves the long-form demonstrations untested; its timings also show that a preferred avatar can still be slower to produce than the fastest rival.

GitHub Pilot Turns AI Security Findings Into 55 Open Source Fixes

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GitHub reported on November 17, 2026, that a security pilot with Microsoft produced 55 fixes across six open source projects as of November 15, 2026. The Codename MDASH trial filtered 9,791 possible issues into 99 private reports, each checked by researchers and accompanied by a proof of concept and suggested fix.

Fourteen of the fixes had already been included in releases. The projects were AutoGPT, Caddy, Express, FastMCP, ImageMagick and Netty. Maintainers decided which findings to fix, how to fix them and when to ship, with reports and patches staying on GitHub.

The design addresses a maintenance problem created by cheaper AI scanning: duplicates and reports that are not actual vulnerabilities already consume maintainer time, while remediation still requires human judgment. GitHub wants researchers to consult maintainers before pointing another model at their projects and to submit patches alongside any findings.

Microsoft’s Codename MDASH system scanned each project with its MAI Cyber model and suggested fixes. A GitHub Security Lab AI triage agent, built on tailored Copilot skills, removed duplicates, checked validity and focused on high-impact bugs. Seven Microsoft FORGE Lab researchers then manually checked every remaining finding before reports went to maintainers through GitHub private vulnerability reporting.

The first fixes shipped about a week after the initial scan, according to GitHub. Caddy’s maintainers fixed all 22 reports they received in a week or less. ImageMagick, maintained by two people, also received 22 reports; in one case, its maintainers pushed a fix in 15 minutes after a summary identified the exact lines of code and showed that an apparently familiar issue was different.

At AutoGPT, a report arrived three days after maintainers shipped a broken check and pointed to the right line. They took the fix further than the suggested patch, wrote it within a day and had it in production less than a week later. Across the pilot, maintainers used some suggested fixes as written and adapted others.

A Netty maintainer described the remaining constraint: “Finding vulnerabilities is no longer our bottleneck. Fixing them is the hard part, and that depends on maintainers.”

GitHub described the project sample as small, the conclusions as early lessons and the manual review as not yet scalable; researchers spent about two days per project checking findings by hand. Low-quality reports still reach maintainers every day.

GitHub also identified private-fork CI/CD testing and maintainer access to cyber-capable models as remaining needs. The tests would verify fixes on private forks, and model access would help maintainers verify a proof of concept before and after remediation. Its recommended standard for AI-generated reports is to check each finding against the project’s existing knowledge, threat model and security policy, report privately, include a suggested fix and let maintainers set the pace.

GitHub plans to bring these lessons into private vulnerability reporting and security advisories. It will offer AI security scans with findings checked before private reporting, plus GitHub Copilot Max, to all alumni projects of the GitHub Secure Open Source Fund and extend the support to future Fund sessions. GitHub and Microsoft are also working to expand researcher review while preserving those checks.

For AI-assisted vulnerability disclosure, the pilot’s trade-off is concrete: automation can reduce a flood of raw findings to a manageable private queue, while expert review and maintainer control determine whether those reports lead to patches. Expansion hinges on preserving that standard without returning the triage burden to maintainers.
